).

4.7.
Step 7. Further iterations of coarse–graining.

The further iterations proceed quite similarly to the
(j_{1}-1)
th–scale iteration; for the most part, to do the
j
th–scale iteration we simply replace
j_{1}-1,j_{1}
throughout by
j,j+1
. We will sketch the
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale (third) iteration to make the pattern clear.

For the first stage of the
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale iteration, the current marked PG path at the start is
\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,2}:b^{0}\to b^{1}\to\cdots\to b^{n+1}
, which we rename
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0}}
.
Letting

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}I_{xy}^{2}}=\{i\in\{3,\dots,n-2\}:b^{i}\text{ lies in a non-incidental $(j_{1}-2)$th--scale hyperplane in $\mathcal{H}_{xy}$}\}

we shift each
b^{i},\,i\in I_{xy}^{2}
, to the
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale grid, creating the updated marked PG path

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}:\hat{b}^{0}\to\hat{b}^{1}\to\cdots\to\hat{b}^{n}\to\hat{b}^{n+1}}.

Removing those
b^{i}
which lie in non-terminal
(j_{1}-1)
th–scale hyperplanes then creates the marked PG path

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,2}:\zeta^{0}\to\zeta^{1}\to\cdots\to\zeta^{n_{2}}\to\zeta^{n_{2}+1}},

in which (recalling (
4.18
))
\zeta^{0}=\hat{x},\,\zeta^{n_{2}+1}=\hat{y}
,
\zeta^{1}
and
\zeta^{n_{2}}
lie in terminal
j_{1}
th–scale hyperplanes,
\zeta^{2},\zeta^{n_{2}-1}
lie in terminal
(j_{1}-1)
th–scale hyperplanes, and
\zeta^{3},\dots,\zeta^{n_{2}-2}
lie in
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale hyperplanes.
Noninteraction of shifts again applies (see Step 4), since if
b^{i}
and
b^{k}
both lie in
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale hyperplanes then there are sandwiching hyperplanes, at least, in between, ensuring
|i-k|\geq 2
. For
N\leq n_{2}
we write
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma^{2,N,full}}
for the portion of
\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}
in the interval (denoted
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}I_{N}^{2}}
) from
(\zeta^{N})_{1}
to
(\zeta^{N+1})_{1}
, and define
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\gamma(2,N)}
by
\zeta^{N}=\hat{b}^{\gamma(2,N)},N\leq n_{2}+1
, analogous to
\gamma(N)
used in the previous iteration.

Similarly to (
4.100
)—(
4.102
) we have

(4.150)

\sum_{i\in I_{xy}}A_{j_{1}-1}^{1}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0},i)\leq\frac{2}{3}\lambda^{j_{1}-1}\Big[t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)\Big],

(4.151)

\delta\mu\Big|\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}\right)-\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0}\right)\Big|\leq\frac{\delta\lambda^{j_{1}-1}}{2}\left[\frac{t\sigma_{r}}{3}+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0})-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)\right],

and

(4.152)

\Big|\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}\right)-\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0}\right)\Big|\leq\lambda^{j_{1}-1}\left[\frac{t\sigma_{r}}{3}+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0})-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)\right].

These lead to a tracking bound like (
4.5
) for the first stage (shifting to the
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale grid) using Lemma
4.6
:

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0})-\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1})<-\sum_{i\in I_{xy}^{2}}A_{j_{1}-1}^{1}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0},i)+\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0}\right)-\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq P\Bigg(\max\Big(\big|\hat{T}(\hat{b}^{i-1},\hat{b}^{i})-h(|\hat{b}^{i}-\hat{b}^{i-1}|)\big|,\big|\hat{T}(b^{i-1},b^{i})-h(|b^{i}-b^{i-1}|)\big|\Big)>\frac{1}{4}A_{j_{1}-1}^{1}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0},i)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\text{ for some $1\leq i\leq n+1$ and $(x,y)\in X_{r}$ with $\{i-1,i\}\cap I_{xy}^{2}\neq\emptyset$};\,\omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq P\Bigg(\big|\hat{T}(v,w)-h(|w-v|)\big|\geq A_{j_{1}-1}^{2}(v,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\text{ for some $(j_{1}-1)$th--scale transition $v\to w$ with }v,w\in G_{r}^{+}\cap\mathbb{L}_{j_{1}-1}\Bigg)

(4.153)

\displaystyle\leq C_{70}\exp\left(-C_{71}\left(\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j_{1}-1}t\right).

As with (
4.3
) and (
4.5
), with (
4.6
) this yields

\displaystyle P\Big(T(x,y)\leq h((y-x)_{1})-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq P\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1})-h((y-x)_{1})\leq-\left(1-2\lambda^{j_{1}}\right)t\sigma_{r}+4\delta\mu\lambda^{j_{1}}\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+3\lambda^{j_{1}}\Big(t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big[\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+2\lambda^{j_{1}-1}\Big(t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big[\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}\right)-\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)\Big)\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg)

(4.154)

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt+c_{32}e^{-c_{33}t}+c_{42}\exp\left(-c_{43}\left(\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j_{1}}t\right)+C_{70}\exp\left(-C_{71}\left(\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j_{1}-1}t\right).

Remark 4.9
.

The second probability here contains three quantities of the form

\lambda^{j+1}\Big(t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big[\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big]\Big),

with certain additional integer coefficients: 2 and 4 in the first quantity, 3 in the second quantity, and 2 in the third.
As noted in Remark
4.8
and after (
4.5
), (
4.6
), and (
4.6
), these represent the accumulated error allocations from the
j_{1}
th,
(j_{1}-1)
th, and (first–stage)
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale iterations, respectively. We have split these out here for clarity; in (
4.6
) and (
4.6
) the first two of the three quantities are combined, giving the terms with coefficients 5 and 7. In general after the first stage of the
j
th–scale iteration (which is the source of the third quantity), the second quantity would represent allocations from all stages
j_{1}-1
through
j+1
. With this in mind we define the accumulated–allocations upper bounds

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathcal{A}_{j}^{1}(\Gamma_{xy})}=

\displaystyle 2\lambda^{j_{1}}\Big(t\sigma_{r}+2\delta\mu\Big[\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=j+1}^{j_{1}-1}3\lambda^{k+1}\Big(t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big[\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{k,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big]\Big)

(4.155)

\displaystyle+2\lambda^{j+1}\Big(t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big[\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big]\Big)

and

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathcal{A}_{j}^{2}(\Gamma_{xy})}=

\displaystyle 2\lambda^{j_{1}}\Big(t\sigma_{r}+2\delta\mu\Big[\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big]\Big)

(4.156)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=j}^{j_{1}-1}3\lambda^{k+1}\Big(t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big[\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{k,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big]\Big),

with
\mathcal{A}_{j}^{1}(\Gamma_{xy})
a valid upper bound after the first stage of the
j
th–scale iteration, and
\mathcal{A}_{j}^{2}(\Gamma_{xy})
valid after the second stage. Comparing (
4.9
) to (
4.5
), in this case we have
j=j_{1}-1
, the sum in (
4.9
) has no terms, and the other two terms on the right in (
4.5
) merge into one. Comparing (
4.9
) to (
4.6
), in this instance also
j=j_{1}-1
, and the sum in (
4.9
) has one term which is merged with the other term in (
4.6
). Equation (
4.9
) may also be compared to (
4.3
), where
j=j_{1}
and the sum in (
4.9
) has no terms.
We can rewrite the second probability in (
4.7
) as

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}\right)-h((y-x)_{1})\leq-t\sigma_{r}+\mathcal{A}_{j_{1}-2}^{1}(\Gamma_{xy})

(4.157)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}\right)-\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)\Big)\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg).

Moving on to the second stage of the
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale iteration, from (
4.7
), using the form (
4.9
), we have similarly to (
4.6
) and (
4.6
)

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Big(T(x,y)\leq h((y-x)_{1})-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq P\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,2}\right)-h((y-x)_{1})\leq-t\sigma_{r}+\mathcal{A}_{j_{1}-2}^{2}(\Gamma_{xy})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,2}\right)-\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)\Big)\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+P\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}\right)-\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,2}\right)\leq-\sum_{N=0}^{n_{2}}S_{j_{1}-1}(\Gamma^{2,N,full})

\displaystyle\qquad\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{3}\lambda^{j_{1}-1}\Big(t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big[\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,2}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\hskip 28.45274pt+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,1}\right)-\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,2}\right)\Big)\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg)

(4.158)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+c_{32}e^{-c_{33}t}+c_{42}\exp\left(-c_{43}\left(\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j_{1}}t\right)+C_{70}\exp\left(-C_{71}\left(\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j_{1}-1}t\right),

the last probability being the tracking–failure probability. We now divide into Cases 1–5 as in the
(j_{1}-1)
th–scale iteration. In each case (excluding 3c) there is an intermediate path
\Gamma^{2,N,int}
in each
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale interval
I_{N}^{2}
, which is slower than the direct path (up to a multiple of log
r
), satisfying

\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}(\Gamma^{2,N,int})\geq\hat{T}(\hat{b}^{\gamma(2,N)},\hat{b}^{\gamma(2,N+1)})-\big(\gamma(2,N+1)-\gamma(2,N)-1\big)c_{29}\log r

assuming
\omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})
, and hence

\sum_{N=0}^{n_{2}}\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}(\Gamma^{2,N,int})\geq\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,2}\right)-(n-n_{2})c_{29}\log r,

and which satisfies deterministic tracking in a form like (
4.113
), sometimes with extra terms in the form of error allocations, as in (
4.6
); this deterministic tracking says that the intermediate path is sufficiently shorter relative to the full path, to within the error given by the allocations. This enables us to bound the last probability in (
4.7
) by a tracking–failure probability of the form

\displaystyle P\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma^{2,N,full}\right)-\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma^{2,N,int}\right)\leq\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma^{2,N,full}\right)-\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma^{2,N,int}\right)-\text{ (allocations)}

(4.159)

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt\text{ for some $N$ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg),

differently for each of the 5 cases; see for example (
4.139
) for the totally–unbowed case. This is then bounded by summing probabilities of form

P\Big(\Big|\hat{T}(v,w)-h(|w-v|)\Big|\geq A)

over all possible links
(v,w)
of paths
\Gamma^{2,N,full},\Gamma^{2,N,int}
for all
N
, using Lemmas
4.6
and
4.7
. The end result of the
(j_{1}-2)
th–scale iteration is that

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Big(T(x,y)\leq h((y-x)_{1})-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq P\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,2}\right)-h((y-x)_{1})\leq-t\sigma_{r}+\mathcal{A}_{j_{1}-2}^{2}(\Gamma_{xy})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-2,2}\right)-\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)\Big)\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg)

(4.160)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+c_{32}e^{-c_{33}t}+\sum_{j=j_{1}-2}^{j_{1}-1}c_{42}\exp\left(-c_{43}\left(\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j+1}t\right).

After all iterations are completed through the
j_{2}
th scale, this becomes

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Big(T(x,y)\leq h((y-x)_{1})-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq P\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{2},2}\right)-h((y-x)_{1})\leq-t\sigma_{r}+\mathcal{A}_{j_{2}}^{2}(\Gamma_{xy})+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{2},2}\right)-\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg)

(4.161)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07182pt+c_{32}e^{-c_{33}t}+\sum_{j=j_{2}}^{j_{1}-1}c_{42}\exp\left(-c_{43}\left(\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j+1}t\right).

We claim that

(4.162)

\mathcal{A}_{j_{2}}^{2}(\Gamma_{xy})+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{2},2}\right)-\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)\Big)\leq\frac{1}{2}t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{2},2}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big).

This says that the (typically negative) second term on the left, representing part of the cumulative effects of tracking, is enough to adequately cancel the (positive) cumulative allocations
\mathcal{A}_{j_{2}}^{2}(\Gamma_{xy})
; see the comments after (
4.6
) and (
4.6
). To prove (
4.162
) we make the subclaim

(4.163)

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}D}:=\max_{j_{2}\leq j\leq j_{1}-1}\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\leq 2\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)+t\sigma_{r}.

Assuming (
4.163
) and assuming
\lambda
satisfies
7\lambda/(1-\lambda)<1/4
we have from the definition (
4.9
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{j_{2}}^{2}(\Gamma_{xy})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{7\lambda}{1-\lambda}\Bigg(t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\bigg[\max_{j_{2}\leq j\leq j_{1}-1}\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\bigg]\Bigg)

(4.164)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1+\delta\mu}{4}t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)

and (
4.162
) follows. To prove (
4.163
) we use (
4.101
) (which generalizes to all
j
in place of
j_{1}-1
) and the fact that, since removing points reduces length, we have
\Upsilon_{Euc}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j,2})\leq\Upsilon_{Euc}(\Gamma_{xy}^{j,1})
for all
j
. This yields that for all
j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j,0}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)+\sum_{k=j+1}^{j_{1}-1}\Big|\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{k,1}\right)-\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{k,0}\right)\Big|

(4.165)

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)+\sum_{k=j+1}^{j_{1}-1}\frac{\lambda^{k+1}}{2\mu}\Big[\frac{t\sigma_{r}}{3}+\delta\mu D\Big]

and therefore

(4.166)

\displaystyle D\leq\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{1}-1,0}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}+\frac{\lambda^{3}}{6\mu(1-\lambda)}t\sigma_{r}+\frac{\delta\lambda^{3}}{2(1-\lambda)}D,

from which (
4.163
) follows, and hence also (
4.162
). The right side of (
4.7
) is therefore bounded above by

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{2},2}\right)-h((y-x)_{1})\leq-\frac{1}{2}t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{2},2}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)

(4.167)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg)+c_{44}\exp\left(-c_{45}\left(\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j_{2}+1}t\right).

4.8.
Step 8. Final marked CG paths.

In
\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{2},2}
, only terminal hyperplanes contain marked points (excluding
\hat{x},\hat{y}
), one at each end of
[\hat{x}_{1},\hat{y}_{1}]
for each scale
j_{2}\leq j\leq j_{1}
. The marked point in the
j
–terminal hyperplane is in the grid
\mathbb{L}_{j}
for all
j
.
As noted in Step 1, the gap between the
j_{2}
–terminal hyperplanes is at least
4\delta^{j_{2}}r
and at most
5\delta^{j_{2}-1}r
. Now
\Gamma_{xy}^{j_{2},2}
is our final CG path, so we rename it

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}}:\,\hat{x}=u^{0}\to\cdots\to u^{R+1}=\hat{y},

where
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}R}=2(j_{1}-j_{2}+1)
.
We also define a projected path with collinear marked points

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Gamma_{xy}^{CG,pr}}:\,v^{0}\to\cdots\to v^{R+1},

where
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}v^{i}}=(u^{i})_{1}e_{1}
is the projection onto the
e_{1}
axis.
For
j_{1}-1\leq j\leq j_{2}
, the links
(u^{j_{1}-j},u^{j_{1}-j+1})
and
(u^{R-j_{1}+j},u^{R-j_{1}+j+1})
each have one end in a
j
–terminal hyperplane and the other in a
(j+1)
–terminal hyperplane; these will be called the
jth–scale links
of
\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}
.
The links
(u^{0},u^{1})
and
(u^{R},u^{R+1})
are called
final links
, and the link
(u^{R/2},u^{R/2+1})
between
j_{2}
–terminal hyperplanes is called a
macroscopic link
. See Figure
3
in Section
4.1
, where
(u^{3},u^{4})
is the macroscopic link, which (by definition of
j_{2}
) always covers at least 1/5 the length of
[\hat{x}_{1},\hat{y}_{1}]
.

We have
\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG,pr}\right)=(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}
so by Lemma
3.6
, since
R\leq 2j_{1}
,

\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)-\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG,pr}\right)\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)-2j_{1}C_{56}.

Therefore, using (
4.64
), the probability in (
4.7
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)-\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)\leq-\frac{1}{3}t\sigma_{r}+\delta\mu\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)-\Big[\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)-\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG,pr}\right)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq P\Bigg(\Upsilon_{\hat{T}}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)-\Upsilon_{h}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)\leq-\frac{1}{3}t\sigma_{r}-\frac{\mu}{2}\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)

(4.168)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\text{ for some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\Bigg).

To bound this we use allocations

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}A_{j}^{CG}(v,w)}=\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{j}\left[t^{*}(v,w)\sigma_{r}+\frac{\delta\mu}{18}\frac{|(w-v)^{*}|^{2}}{|(w-v)_{1}|}\right]

for
j
th–scale links,
A_{j_{1}}^{CG}(v,w)
for final links, and
A_{j_{2}}^{CG}(v,w)
for macroscopic links. From (
4.80
) and (
4.81
) we have

\frac{\delta\mu}{18}\max_{k\leq R+1}\frac{|(u^{k})^{*}|^{2}}{r\sigma_{r}}\leq\frac{\delta t}{3}+\frac{\delta\mu}{12\sigma_{r}}\sum_{i=1}^{R+1}\frac{|(u^{i-1}-u^{i})^{*}|^{2}}{|(u^{i-1}-u^{i})_{1}|}

Therefore using also (
4.21
) the total of these for all links in a path
\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}
satisfies

\displaystyle A_{j_{2}}^{CG}

\displaystyle(u^{R/2},u^{R/2+1})+A_{j_{1}}^{CG}(u^{0},u^{1})+A_{j_{1}}^{CG}(u^{R},u^{R+1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=j_{2}}^{j_{1}-1}\Big[A_{j+1}^{CG}(u^{j_{1}-j},u^{j_{1}-j+1})+A_{j+1}^{CG}(u^{R-j_{1}+j},u^{R-j_{1}+j+1})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{j=j_{2}-1}^{j_{1}-1}\frac{\lambda^{j+1}}{4}\left[\left(\frac{t}{3}+2\frac{\delta\mu}{18}\max_{k\leq R+1}\frac{|(u^{k})^{*}|^{2}}{r\sigma_{r}}\right)\sigma_{r}+\frac{\delta\mu}{18}\sum_{i=1}^{R+1}\frac{|(u^{i-1}-u^{i})^{*}|^{2}}{|(u^{i-1}-u^{i})_{1}|}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=j_{2}}^{j_{1}}\frac{1}{3}\lambda^{j}\left(1+2\delta\right)t\sigma_{r}+\sum_{j=j_{2}}^{j_{1}}\lambda^{j}\left(\frac{\delta\mu}{3}+\frac{\delta\mu}{6}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{R+1}\frac{|(u^{i-1}-u^{i})^{*}|^{2}}{|(u^{i-1}-u^{i})_{1}|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}t\sigma_{r}+\frac{3\delta\mu\lambda}{2(1-\lambda)}\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)

(4.169)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}t\sigma_{r}+\frac{\mu}{2}\Big(\Upsilon_{Euc}\left(\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}\right)-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big).

Therefore the last probability in (
4.8
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{j_{1}}P\bigg(\Big|\hat{T}(u^{i-1},u^{i})-h(|u^{i-1}-u^{i}|)\Big|\geq A_{j+1}^{CG}(u^{i-1},u^{i})\text{ for some $j$th--scale link}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(u^{i-1},u^{i})\text{ of $\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}$ and some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+P\bigg(\Big|\hat{T}(u^{i-1},u^{i})-h(|u^{i-1}-u^{i}|)\Big|\geq A_{j_{2}}^{CG}(u^{i-1},u^{i})\text{ for some macroscopic link}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(u^{i-1},u^{i})\text{ of $\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}$ and some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+P\bigg(\Big|\hat{T}(u^{i-1},u^{i})-h(|u^{i-1}-u^{i}|)\Big|\geq A_{j_{1}}^{CG}(u^{i-1},u^{i})\text{ for some final link}

(4.170)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(u^{i-1},u^{i})\text{ of $\Gamma_{xy}^{CG}$ and some }(x,y)\in X_{r};\ \omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})\cup J^{(1c)}\bigg).

Considering the probability for final links
(v,w)
, the number of possible such links in
G_{r}^{+}
is at most
c_{46}r^{2d}
and for each such link we have from Lemma
3.2
and (
4.16
)

\displaystyle P\Big(\Big|\hat{T}(v,w)

\displaystyle-h(|w-v|)\Big|\geq A_{j_{1}}^{CG}(v,w)\Big)\leq C_{44}\exp\left(-C_{45}\frac{A_{j_{1}}^{CG}(v,w)}{\sigma(2\delta^{j_{1}}r)}\right)

(4.171)

\displaystyle\leq c_{47}\exp\left(-\frac{c_{48}}{j_{1}}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j_{1}}t\right)\leq c_{47}\exp\left(-c_{49}t\log r\right).

The probabilities for
j
th–scale and macroscopic links can be bounded using minor modifications of Lemma
4.6
, showing that (
4.8
) (and hence also the probability on the right in (
4.7
)) is bounded above by

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{j_{1}}

\displaystyle c_{50}\exp\left(-c_{51}\left(\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j}t\right)+c_{50}\exp\left(-c_{51}\frac{\lambda}{7\delta^{\chi_{1}}}t\right)+c_{46}c_{47}r^{2d}\exp\left(-c_{49}t\log r\right)

(4.172)

\displaystyle\leq c_{52}e^{-c_{53}t}.

With (
4.7
) this completes the proof of (
1.13
). As noted after Theorem (
1.3
), the downward–deviations part of (
1.12
) is a consequence, so the proof of the downward–deviations part of Theorem (
1.3
) is complete.

5.
Proof of Theorem
1.6
for fixed
(x,y)
.

We move on to step (2) of the strategy in Remark
1.7
.
As with the proof of Theorem
1.3
(downward deviations), there are simpler cases which can be proved from Lemma
3.2
and do not require Theorem
1.3
. These we can handle uniformly over
(x,y)
; after dealing with these, in Lemma
5.1
we will handle the main case for the moment only for fixed
(x,y)
.

Let

(5.1)

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r_{0}}=\frac{c_{0}r}{(\log r)^{1/(1+\chi_{1})}},

with
c_{0}
to be specified;
we will consider separately short geodesics (meaning
|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|\leq r_{0}
) and longer ones.
When
\Gamma_{xy}\not\subset G_{r,s}\text{ for some }x,y\in\mathbb{V}\cap G_{r}(K)
with
|(y-x)^{*}|\leq(y-x)_{1}
, taking
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}u}
to be the first vertex in
\Gamma_{xy}
outside
G_{r,s}
, we see that one of the following cases must hold.

Transverse wandering cases (see Figure
10
):

(i)

(
non-transverse wandering
) there exist
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\hat{x},\hat{y},\hat{u}}\in q\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\hat{x},\hat{y}\in G_{r}(K),\hat{u}\notin G_{r,s}
with
d(\hat{u},G_{r,s})\leq 5
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}x,y,u}\in\mathbb{V}
with

\max(|x-\hat{x}|,|y-\hat{y}|,|u-\hat{u}|)\leq 5\sqrt{d-1},\quad u\in\Gamma_{xy},\quad\hat{u}_{1}\notin[\hat{x}_{1},\hat{y}_{1}],

(ii)

(
wandering by short geodesics
) there exist
\hat{x},\hat{y},\hat{u}\in q\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\hat{x},\hat{y}\in G_{r}(K),\hat{u}\notin G_{r,s}
with
d(\hat{u},G_{r,s})\leq 5
, and
x,y,u\in\mathbb{V}
with

\max(|x-\hat{x}|,|y-\hat{y}|,|u-\hat{u}|)\leq 5\sqrt{d-1},\quad u\in\Gamma_{xy},\quad\hat{u}_{1}\in[\hat{x}_{1},\hat{y}_{1}],\quad|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|\leq r_{0},

(iii)

(
large wandering
)
s>c_{1}(\log r)^{1/2}
and there exist
\hat{x},\hat{y},\hat{u}\in q\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\hat{x},\hat{y}\in G_{r}(K),\hat{u}\notin G_{r,s}
with
d(\hat{u},G_{r,s})\leq 5
, and
x,y,u\in\mathbb{V}
with

(5.2)

\max(|x-\hat{x}|,|y-\hat{y}|,|u-\hat{u}|)\leq 5\sqrt{d-1},\quad|y-x|>r_{0},\quad u\in\Gamma_{xy},\quad u_{1}\in[x_{1},y_{1}],

(iv)

(
moderate wandering
)
s\leq c_{1}(\log r)^{1/2}
and there exist
x,y,u\in\mathbb{V}
with

(5.3)

x,y\in G_{r}(K),\quad u\notin G_{r,s},\quad d(u,G_{r,s})\leq 2\quad|y-x|>r_{0},\quad u\in\Gamma_{xy},\quad u_{1}\in[x_{1},y_{1}],

where
c_{1}
is to be specified. In all cases (i)–(iv) we assume
|(y-x)^{*}|\leq(y-x)_{1}
.

Figure 10.
The inner box
G_{r}(K)
has height
2K\Delta_{r}
, and the outer box
G_{r,s}
has height
2s\Delta_{r}
. The dotted line shows
\Gamma_{xy}
in transverse wandering case (i), the solid line shows case (ii), and the dashed line shows cases (iii) and (iv), for large and small
s
, respectively.

Cases (i)—(iii) are the ones which do not require Theorem
1.3
and can be proved from Lemma
3.2
. This is because the exponent obtained from that lemma is at least of order
\log r
, meaning it dominates the entropy from the number of possible choices of
\hat{x},\hat{y},\hat{u}
.

It follows from routine geometry that under (i), for
C_{34}
from (
1.15
) we have

|\hat{u}-\hat{x}|+|\hat{y}-\hat{u}|-|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|\geq d(G_{r}(K),G_{r,s}^{c})\geq\begin{cases}s^{2}\sigma_{r}\log r&\text{if }s\leq(C_{34}\log r)^{1/2},\\
s^{2}\sigma_{r}&\text{if }(C_{34}\log r)^{1/2}<s\leq r/\Delta_{r},\\
s\Delta_{r}&\text{if }s>r/\Delta_{r}\end{cases}

so also, using Lemma
3.6
,

(5.4)

h(|\hat{u}-\hat{x}|)+h(|\hat{y}-\hat{u}|)-h(|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|)\geq\begin{cases}\frac{\mu}{2}s^{2}\sigma_{r}\log r&\text{if }s\leq(C_{34}\log r)^{1/2},\\
\frac{\mu}{2}s^{2}\sigma_{r}&\text{if }(C_{34}\log r)^{1/2}<s\leq r/\Delta_{r},\\
\frac{\mu}{2}s\Delta_{r}&\text{if }s>r/\Delta_{r},\end{cases}

while

\Big|\hat{T}(\hat{x},\hat{u})+\hat{T}(\hat{u},\hat{y})-\hat{T}(\hat{x},\hat{y})\Big|\leq M(\hat{x})+M(\hat{y})+M(\hat{u}).

Therefore one of the following 6 quantities

\Big|\hat{T}(\hat{x},\hat{u})-h(|\hat{u}-\hat{x}|)\Big|,\quad\Big|\hat{T}(\hat{u},\hat{y})-h(|\hat{y}-\hat{u}|)\Big|,\quad\Big|\hat{T}(\hat{x},\hat{y})-h(|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|)\Big|,\quad M(\hat{x}),\ \ M(\hat{y}),\ \ M(\hat{u})

exceeds 1/6 of the right side of (
5.4
). There are at most
c_{2}s^{d}r^{2d}
possible values of
(\hat{x},\hat{y},\hat{u})
under (i), so from Lemmas
3.1
and
3.2
, provided
C_{34}
is sufficiently large we have

(5.5)

\displaystyle P\Big((i)\text{ holds}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c_{2}s^{d}r^{2d}\cdot C_{44}\exp\left(-c_{3}\frac{s^{2}\sigma_{r}\log r}{\sigma(2r)}\right)\leq c_{4}e^{-c_{5}s^{2}}&\text{if }s\leq(C_{34}\log r)^{1/2}\\
c_{2}s^{d}r^{2d}\cdot C_{44}\exp\left(-c_{3}\frac{s^{2}\sigma_{r}}{\sigma(2r)}\right)\leq c_{4}e^{-c_{5}s^{2}}&\text{if }(C_{34}\log r)^{1/2}<s\leq r/\Delta_{r},\\
c_{2}s^{d}r^{2d}\cdot C_{44}\exp\left(-c_{3}\frac{s\Delta_{r}}{\sigma(s\Delta_{r})}\right)\leq c_{4}e^{-c_{5}s\Delta_{r}/\sigma(s\Delta_{r})}&\text{if }s>r/\Delta_{r}.\end{cases}

Under (ii) we have for some
c_{6}

|\hat{u}-\hat{x}|+|\hat{y}-\hat{u}|-|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|\geq\begin{cases}c_{6}s^{2}\Delta_{r}^{2}/r_{0}&\text{if }s\leq r_{0}/\Delta_{r},\\
c_{6}s\Delta_{r}&\text{if }s>r_{0}/\Delta_{r}.\end{cases}

Further, for
s\leq r/\Delta_{r}
we have
1/s\Delta_{r}\sigma(s\Delta_{r})\leq c_{7}/\Delta_{r}^{2}
or equivalently
s\Delta_{r}/\sigma(s\Delta_{r})\geq c_{7}s^{2}
.
Hence similarly to (i), provided we take
c_{0}
large in (
5.1
),

(5.6)

\displaystyle P\Big((ii)\text{ holds}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c_{1}s^{d}r^{2d}\cdot C_{44}\exp\left(-c_{8}\frac{s^{2}r\sigma_{r}}{r_{0}\sigma(r_{0})}\right)\leq c_{9}e^{-c_{9}s^{2}\log r}&\text{if }s\leq r_{0}/\Delta_{r}\\
c_{1}s^{d}r^{2d}\cdot C_{44}\exp\left(-c_{8}\frac{s\Delta_{r}}{\sigma(s\Delta_{r})}\right)\leq c_{9}e^{-c_{9}s^{2}}&\text{if }r_{0}/\Delta_{r}<s\leq r/\Delta_{r},\\
c_{1}s^{d}r^{2d}\cdot C_{44}\exp\left(-c_{8}\frac{s\Delta_{r}}{\sigma(s\Delta_{r})}\right)\leq c_{9}e^{-c_{9}s\Delta_{r}/\sigma(s\Delta_{r})}&\text{if }s>r/\Delta_{r}.\end{cases}

Under (iii) we have

|\hat{u}-\hat{x}|+|\hat{y}-\hat{u}|-|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|\geq\begin{cases}c_{11}s^{2}\sigma_{r}&\text{if }c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\leq s\leq r/\Delta_{r},\\
c_{11}s\Delta_{r}&\text{if }s>r/\Delta_{r}.\end{cases}

Hence as in (i) and (ii) we get

(5.7)

\displaystyle P\Big((iii)\text{ holds}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}c_{1}s^{d}r^{2d}\cdot C_{44}\exp\left(-c_{12}\frac{s^{2}\sigma_{r}}{\sigma(2r)}\right)\leq c_{13}e^{-c_{14}s^{2}}&\text{if }c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\leq s\leq r/\Delta_{r}\\
c_{1}s^{d}r^{2d}\cdot C_{44}\exp\left(-c_{12}\frac{s\Delta_{r}}{\sigma(s\Delta_{r})}\right)\leq c_{13}e^{-c_{14}s\Delta_{r}/\sigma(s\Delta_{r})}&\text{if }s>r/\Delta_{r}.\end{cases}

To deal with (iv) and complete the proof for fixed
(x,y)
, we have the following. We note the difference from Lemma
4.2
, which covered many
x,y
simultaneously but considered only larger transverse wandering, of order
\Delta_{r}\log r
or more.

Lemma 5.1
.

Suppose
\mathbb{G}=(\mathbb{V},\mathbb{E})
and
\{\eta_{e},e\in\mathbb{E}\}
satisfy A1, A2, and A3. There exist
C_{i}
such that for all
K\geq C_{74}
and all
x,y\in G_{r}(K)
with
|(y-x)^{*}|\leq(y-x)_{1}
,

(5.8)

\displaystyle P\Big(\Gamma_{xy}\not\subset G_{r,s}\Big)\leq C_{75}e^{-C_{76}s^{2}}\quad\text{for all }C_{77}K\leq s\leq C_{78}(\log r)^{1/2}.

Proof.

Fix
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}x,y}\in G_{r}(K)
and let
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r_{1}}=(y-x)_{1}
. We consider first the case of
r_{1}<r/2
. Let
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}m}\geq 0
satisfy
mr_{1}\leq x_{1}<y_{1}\leq(m+2)r_{1}
, and for
C_{22}
from (
1.10
) let

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}s_{1}}=2C_{22}s\left(\frac{r}{2r_{1}}\right)^{(1+\chi_{1})/2},\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}G_{r,2s}^{(m)}}=[mr_{1},(m+2)r_{1}]\times 2s\Delta_{r}\mathfrak{B}_{d-1},

the latter being a slice of
G_{r,2s}
. Define
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}s_{2}}
by
2s\Delta_{r}=s_{2}\Delta_{2r_{1}}
.
Then
s_{2}\geq s
, and from (
1.10
) we have
s_{2}\geq s_{1}
, and
G_{r,2s}^{(m)}
is a translate of
G_{2r_{1}}(s_{2})
.

In view of (
5.5
) and (
5.6
), we need only consider
(x,y,u)
as in (
5.3
); in particular
r_{1}\geq r_{0}/2
.
Here we have for some
c_{0}

|u-x|+|y-u|-|y-x|\geq c_{0}s_{2}^{2}\sigma(2r_{1})\quad\text{and hence}\quad h(|u-x|)+h(|y-u|)-h(|y-x|)\geq\frac{c_{0}\mu}{2}s_{2}^{2}\sigma(2r_{1}),

so as in transverse wandering cases (i)–(iii), since
u\in\Gamma_{xy}
one of the quantities

h(|u-x|)-T(x,u),\quad h(|y-u|)-T(u,y),\quad T(x,y)-h(|y-x|)

must exceed
c_{0}\mu s_{2}^{2}\sigma(2r_{1})/6
. It follows from (
1.11
) and the downward–deviations part of Theorem
1.3
(with
\epsilon,r,K,t
there taken as
1/2,2r_{1},Ks_{2}/2s,c_{0}\mu s_{2}^{2}/6
here) that for
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}x_{0}}=x-mr_{1}e_{1},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}y_{0}}=y-mr_{1}e_{1}
we have

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma_{xy}\text{ contains a vertex $u$ as in \eqref{twiv}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq P\bigg(\max\Big(h(|u-x|)-T(x,u),h(|y-u|)-T(u,y)\Big)\geq\frac{c_{0}\mu}{6}s_{2}^{2}\sigma(2r_{1})\text{ for some $u\in G_{r,2s}^{(m)}$}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+P\bigg(T(x,y)-h(|y-x|)\geq\frac{c_{0}\mu}{6}s_{2}^{2}\sigma(2r_{1})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq P\bigg(\max\Big(h(|\tilde{u}-x_{0}|)-T(x_{0},\tilde{u}),h(|y_{0}-\tilde{u}|)-T(\tilde{u},y_{0})\Big)\geq\frac{c_{0}\mu}{6}s_{2}^{2}\sigma(2r_{1})\text{ for some $\tilde{u}\in G_{2r_{1}}(s_{2})$}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+P\bigg(T(x,y)-h(|y-x|)\geq\frac{c_{0}\mu}{6}s_{2}^{2}\sigma(2r_{1})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C_{27}e^{-c_{1}s_{2}^{2}}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\leq C_{27}e^{-c_{1}s^{2}}.

∎

This completes the proof of Theorem
1.6
for fixed
(x,y)
, and thus of (2) in Remark
1.7
.

6.
Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

We move to (3) of Remark
1.7
.
We begin with an extension of a consequence of Theorem
1.3
, removing the requirement that
y
lie in the same tube
G_{r}(K)
as
x
, to create a bound on the probability of a fast “hyperplane–block to hyperplane” passage time. Define

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Lambda_{r}}=[0,2]\times[-\Delta_{r},\Delta_{r}]^{d-1}.

Lemma 6.1
.

Suppose
\mathbb{G}=(\mathbb{V},\mathbb{E})
and
\{\eta_{e},e\in\mathbb{E}\}
satisfy A1, A2, and A3. There exist constants
C_{i}
such that for all
r\geq C_{79}
and
t\geq C_{80}
,

(6.1)

P\Big(T(x,y)\leq h(r)-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $x\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\mathbb{V}$ and $y\in H_{r}^{+}$}\Big)\leq C_{81}e^{-C_{82}t}.

Proof.

It is enough to consider
y\in H_{[r,r+2]}
and
t\leq h(r)/\sigma_{r}
. We first deal with large
|y^{*}|
. With
c_{0}
to be specified we have

\displaystyle P\Bigg(

\displaystyle T(x,y)\leq h(r)-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $x\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\mathbb{V}$ and $y\in H_{[r,r+2]}$
with $|y^{*}|>c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}$}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}P\Bigg(T(x,y)\leq h(r)-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $x\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\mathbb{V}$ and $y\in H_{[r,r+2]}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\text{with $2^{k-1}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}<|y^{*}|\leq 2^{k}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}$}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}P\Bigg(\hat{T}(\hat{x},\hat{y})\leq h(r)-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $\hat{x}\in\Lambda_{r}\cap q\mathbb{Z}^{d}$ and $\hat{y}\in H_{r}\cap q\mathbb{Z}^{d}$}

(6.2)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\text{with $2^{k-1}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}<|\hat{y}^{*}|\leq 2^{k}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}$}\Bigg).

For
c_{9}
from (
4.20
), let
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k_{0}}=\max\{k:2^{k}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}\leq c_{9}r\}
.
For
k\leq k_{0}
, and for
\hat{x},\hat{y}
of the last event, we have from (
4.20
) and Lemma
3.6

(6.3)

|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|\geq r+\frac{|\hat{y}^{*}|^{2}}{3r}\geq r+\frac{2^{2k}c_{0}^{2}\sigma_{r}\log r}{12}\quad\text{so}\quad h(|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|)\geq h(r)+\frac{\mu c_{0}^{2}}{24}2^{2k}\sigma_{r}\log r,

and the number of allowed
\hat{x},\hat{y}
is at most
c_{1}(2^{k}(\log r)^{1/2})^{d-1}\Delta_{r}^{2(d-1)}\leq 2^{kd}r^{2d}
. Therefore by Lemma
3.2
, provided
c_{0}
is large, the sum up to
k_{0}
on the right side of (
6
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}P

\displaystyle\Bigg(\hat{T}(\hat{x},\hat{y})\leq h(|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|)-t\sigma_{r}-\frac{\mu c_{0}^{2}}{24}2^{2k}\sigma_{r}\log r\text{ for some $\hat{x}\in\Lambda_{r}\cap q\mathbb{Z}^{d}$ and $\hat{y}\in H_{r}\cap q\mathbb{Z}^{d}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\text{with $2^{k-1}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}<|\hat{y}^{*}|\leq 2^{k}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}$}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}2^{kd}r^{2d}C_{44}\exp\left(-C_{45}\frac{\sigma_{r}}{\sigma(2r)}\left(t+2^{2k}\frac{\mu c_{0}^{2}}{24}\log r\right)\right)

(6.4)

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}e^{-c_{3}t}.

For
k>k_{0}
, in place of (
6.3
) we have

|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|\geq r+c_{4}|\hat{y}^{*}|\geq r+c_{4}2^{k-1}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}\quad\text{so}\quad h(|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|)\geq h(r)+\frac{c_{4}c_{0}\mu}{4}2^{k}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r},

and we obtain similarly that the sum from
k_{0}+1
to
\infty
on the right side of (
6
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{\infty}P

\displaystyle\Bigg(\hat{T}(\hat{x},\hat{y})\leq h(|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|)-t\sigma_{r}-\frac{c_{4}c_{0}\mu}{4}2^{k}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}\text{ for some $\hat{x}\in\Lambda_{r}\cap q\mathbb{Z}^{d}$ and $\hat{y}\in H_{r}\cap q\mathbb{Z}^{d}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\text{with $2^{k-1}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}<|\hat{y}^{*}|\leq 2^{k}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}$}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{\infty}2^{kd}r^{2d}C_{44}\exp\left(-\frac{C_{45}}{\sigma(r+2^{k}c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r})}\left(t\sigma_{r}+\frac{c_{4}c_{0}\mu}{4}2^{k}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{5}\exp\left(-\frac{c_{6}(t\sigma_{r}+r)}{\sigma(2r)}\right)

(6.5)

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}e^{-c_{3}t}.

The last inequality uses
t\leq h(r)/\sigma_{r}
.

We now deal with the remaining case
|y^{*}|\leq c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}
.
For
C_{26}
from Theorem
1.3
, we take
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}K}=\sqrt{t/C_{26}(d-1)}
to be specified and subdivide
H_{[r,r+2]}
into blocks

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}Y_{\mathbf{m}}}=[r,r+2]\times\prod_{i=1}^{d-1}[(m_{i}-1)K\Delta_{r},m_{i}K\Delta_{r}],\quad\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}.

Let
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}z_{\mathbf{m}}}
denote the center point of
Y_{\mathbf{m}}
and let

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathfrak{M}}=\Big\{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}:0\notin Y_{\mathbf{m}},\,\exists y\in Y_{\mathbf{m}}\text{ with }|y^{*}|\leq c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}\Big\}.

Given
\mathbf{m}\in\mathfrak{M}
, there exists a cylinder
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}}
, with axis containing
\Pi_{0z_{\mathbf{m}}}
, radius
2\sqrt{d-1}K\Delta_{r}
, and length
2r
, which contains
\Lambda_{r}
and
Y_{\mathbf{m}}
. See Figure
11
. Further,
d_{\infty}(Y_{\mathbf{m}},[r,r+2]\times\{0\})
is a positive integer multiple of
K\Delta_{r}
, and for
x\in\Lambda_{r}
and
y\in Y_{\mathbf{m}}
satisfying
d_{\infty}(Y_{\mathbf{m}},[r,r+2]\times\{0\})=jK\Delta_{r}
for some
j
, we have using (
4.20
) and Lemma
3.6
(i) that

|y-x|\geq r+\frac{(jK\Delta_{r})^{2}}{3r}=r+\frac{(jK)^{2}}{3}\sigma_{r}\quad\text{and hence}\quad h(|y-x|)\geq h(r)+\frac{\mu(jK)^{2}}{6}\sigma_{r}.

Figure 11.
The block
\Lambda_{r}
in
H_{0}
(“fattened” slightly to thickness 2), the similar block
Y_{\mathbf{m}}
of
H_{r}
, and the cylinder
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}}
containing both, for
\mathbf{m}=(0,1)
. The gray blocks in
H_{r}
are those with
j=1
.

The definition of
K
says
t=C_{26}(d-1)K^{2}
, so
by rotational invariance we can apply Theorem
1.3
(for downward deviations) to the cylinder
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}}
and obtain

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Big(T(x,y)\leq h(r)-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $x\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\mathbb{V}$ and $y\in Y_{\mathbf{m}}$}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq P\left(T(x,y)\leq h(|y-x|)-\left(t+\frac{\mu(jK)^{2}}{6}\right)\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $x\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\mathbb{V}$ and $y\in Y_{\mathbf{m}}$}\right)

(6.6)

\displaystyle\leq C_{27}\exp\left(-C_{28}\left(t+\frac{\mu(jK)^{2}}{6}\right)\right).

Summing over
j\leq{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}j_{0}}=\max\{j:jK\Delta_{r}\leq c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}\}
gives

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Big(T(x,y)\leq h(r)-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $x\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\mathbb{V},y\in H_{[r,r+2]}$ with
$|y^{*}|\leq c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}\Delta_{r}$}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq P\Big(T(x,y)\leq h(r)-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $x\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\mathbb{V},y\in Y_{\mathbf{m}}$,
and $\mathbf{m}\in\mathfrak{M}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\text{with $d_{\infty}(Y_{\mathbf{m}},[r,r+2]\times\{0\})\leq j_{0}K\Delta_{r}$}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{j_{0}}P\bigg(T(x,y)\leq h(r)-t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $x\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\mathbb{V},y\in Y_{\mathbf{m}}$, and $\mathbf{m}\in\mathfrak{M}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\text{with $d_{\infty}(Y_{\mathbf{m}},[r,r+2]\times\{0\})=jK\Delta_{r}$}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{j_{0}}(2j+2)^{d-1}C_{27}\exp\left(-C_{28}\left(t+\frac{\mu(jK)^{2}}{6}\right)\right)

(6.7)

\displaystyle\leq c_{7}e^{-c_{8}t}.

With (
6
), (
6
), and (
6
) this completes the proof of Lemma
6.1
.
∎

Define the “small box”

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathcal{G}_{r}}=[0,r]\times[-\Delta_{r},\Delta_{r}]^{d-1}.

We now tile a region of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with translates of
\mathcal{G}_{r}
. Fix
k,K
to be specified and let
j={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}j(r,k,K)}
be the least
j
for which
(2j+1)\Delta_{r}\geq K\Delta(kr)
. Define the “big box”

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathcal{G}^{+}}=[0,kr]\times[-(2j+1)\Delta_{r},(2j+1)\Delta_{r}]^{d-1},

so the width of
\mathcal{G}^{+}
is near
2K\Delta(kr)
.
For
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
let
\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{m}},\Lambda_{r,\mathbf{m}}
be
\mathcal{G}_{r},\Lambda_{r}
translated by
(rm_{1},2\Delta_{r}\mathbf{m}^{*})
, and let
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathcal{M}}=\{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{m}}\subset\mathcal{G}^{+}\}
; see Figure
12
. Then the number of small boxes comprising
\mathcal{G}^{+}
is

|\mathcal{M}|=k(2j+1)^{d-1}\leq 2k\left(\frac{K\Delta(kr)}{\Delta_{r}}\right)^{d-1}\leq c_{0}K^{d-1}k^{1+(d-1)\chi_{2}},

and we have
\mathcal{G}^{+}\supset G_{kr,K}\cap H_{[0,kr]}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{m}}(t)
be the event in (
6.1
) translated to
\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{m}}
, that is, we replace
\Lambda_{r}
with
\Lambda_{r,\mathbf{m}}
and
H_{r}^{+}
with
H_{(m_{1}+1)r}^{+}
. Fix
C
to be specified; when
\omega\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{m}}(C\log k)
we say
\Lambda_{r,\mathbf{m}}
is a
fast source
. Let
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathcal{L}(r)}=(h(r)-r\mu)/\sigma_{r}
, so

(6.8)

0\leq\mathcal{L}(r)\leq C_{46}\log r

by Proposition
3.3
.

Figure 12.
The big box
\mathcal{G}^{+}
, of size
kr\times(4j+2)\Delta_{r}\approx kr\times K\Delta(kr)
, here with
k=5,j=2
. Each small box
\mathcal{G}_{r,\mathbf{m}}
has size
r\times 2\Delta_{r}
.
\Lambda_{r,(3,1)}
is a fast source if some path like the dashed one is “sufficiently fast” relative to
h(r)
.

We now consider the passage time between the ends of the big box. If
\Gamma_{xy}\subset G_{kr,K}
then
\Gamma_{0,kre_{1}}
must intersect at least
k
of the regions
\Lambda_{r,\mathbf{m}}
, one for each
0\leq m_{1}<k
. If there are no fast sources, this means
T(0,kre_{1})>kr\mu+k\mathcal{L}(r)\sigma_{r}-C\sigma_{r}k\log k
. Provided
K
is large, it then follows from Lemmas
5.1
and
6.1
that

\displaystyle P\Big(T(0,kre_{1})\leq kr\mu+k\mathcal{L}(r)\sigma_{r}-C\sigma_{r}k\log k\Big)

\displaystyle\leq P\Big(\Gamma_{0,kre_{1}}\not\subset G_{kr,K}\Big)+P\Big(\cup_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{m}}(C\log k)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{75}e^{-C_{76}K^{2}}+C_{81}|\mathcal{M}|e^{-C_{82}C\log k}

(6.9)

\displaystyle\leq C_{75}e^{-C_{76}K^{2}}+c_{1}K^{d-1}k^{1+(d-1)\chi_{2}}e^{-C_{82}C\log k}.

By taking
K
large and then
C
large, we can make the right side of (
6
) less than 1/2 for all
k\geq 3
. On the other hand, from (
1.11
) for large
c>1
we have

P\Big(T(0,kre_{1})\leq kr\mu+\mathcal{L}(kr)\sigma(kr)+c\sigma(kr)\Big)\geq 1-C_{24}e^{-C_{25}c}>\frac{1}{2}.

From this and (
6
) it follows that

(6.10)

(\mathcal{L}(kr)+c)\sigma(kr)>k(\mathcal{L}(r)-C\log k)\sigma_{r}\quad\text{for all $r$ large and }k\geq 3.

Fix
k\geq 3
large enough so
k^{1-\chi_{2}}\geq 3C_{23}c
, with
C_{23}
from (
1.10
), and, to get a contradiction, suppose there exists
r
arbitrarily large with
\mathcal{L}(r)\geq 2C\log k
. By (
1.10
) and (
6.10
) we then have

\mathcal{L}(kr)\geq\frac{\sigma_{r}}{\sigma(kr)}\frac{k\mathcal{L}(r)}{2}-c\geq\frac{k^{1-\chi_{2}}}{3C_{23}}\mathcal{L}(r)\geq c\mathcal{L}(r).

Then iteration and (
6.8
) give that for all
n\geq 1
,
C_{46}(n\log k+\log r)\geq\mathcal{L}(k^{n}r)\geq c^{n}\mathcal{L}(r)
. Since this is false for large
n
, we must have
\mathcal{L}(r)<2C\log k
for all large
r
, so
\mathcal{L}(\cdot)
is bounded, which completes the proof of Theorem
1.5
, and thus (3) of Remark
1.7
.

7.
Proof of Theorem
1.3
—upward deviations.

We move on to (4) of Remark
1.7
. The proof is similar to the LPP proof for
d=2
in (
[
8
]
Proposition 10.1).
As with downward deviations, we need only consider
x,y
as in (
4.18
). Let
\mathcal{H}_{xy}
be as in the downward–deviations proof, and

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathcal{H}_{xy}^{ter}}=\{\text{all terminal hyperplanes in }\mathcal{H}_{xy}\},

so
\mathcal{H}_{xy}^{ter}=\{H_{s_{1}},\dots,H_{s_{n}}\}
for some
n,s_{1},\dots,s_{n}
depending on
(x,y)
. Note that
\mathcal{H}_{xy}^{ter}
does not depend on
\Gamma_{xy}
; the
j
–terminal hyperplane at each end of
[\hat{x}_{1},\hat{y}_{1}]
is just the second–closest
j
th–scale hyperplane to the endpoint. We form a marked PG path

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Omega_{xy}:\hat{x}=u^{0}\to\cdots\to u^{n+1}=\hat{y}}

by taking
u^{i}
as the closest point to
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w^{i}}=\Pi_{\hat{x}\hat{y}}\cap H_{s_{i}}
in the
j
th–scale grid, when
H_{s_{i}}
is a
j
–terminal hyperplane; this means
|u^{i}-w^{i}|\leq\sqrt{d-1}\beta^{j}\Delta_{r}
and
n\leq 2j_{1}
(from (
4.16
).) Thus the CG approximation gets coarser as we move away from the endpoints of the interval. See Figure
3
.

We proceed generally as in Step 8 of the proof for downward deviations. Let
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w_{\perp}^{i}}
be the orthogonal projection of
u^{i}
into
\Pi_{\hat{x}\hat{y}}
. We use the terminology
j
th–scale link, final link, and macroscopic link from there. For
j\leq j_{1}
, for a
j
th–scale link
(u^{i-1},u^{i})
we have

(7.1)

\displaystyle|u^{i}-u^{i-1}|\leq|w_{\perp}^{i}-w_{\perp}^{i-1}|+\frac{(|u^{i-1}-w_{\perp}^{i-1}|+|u^{i}-w_{\perp}^{i}|)^{2}}{\delta^{j}r}\leq|w_{\perp}^{i}-w_{\perp}^{i-1}|+\frac{2(d-1)\beta^{2j}\Delta_{r}^{2}}{\delta^{j}r}.

Further, the angle
\alpha
between
e_{1}
and
\hat{y}-\hat{x}
satisfies
\tan\alpha\leq 2K\Delta_{r}/\epsilon r
and therefore

(7.2)

|w^{i}-w_{\perp}^{i}|\leq c_{0}\frac{K\Delta_{r}}{\epsilon r}|u^{i}-w^{i}|\leq c_{1}\frac{K\beta^{j}}{\epsilon}\sigma_{r},

and similarly for
i-1
in place of
i
. We have also
\delta^{j}r/2\leq(u^{i}-u^{i-1})_{1}\leq 2\delta^{j}r
so from (
4.20
) and the bound on
\alpha
we also get

|w^{i}-w^{i-1}|-(w^{i}-w^{i-1})_{1}=\frac{(w^{i}-w^{i-1})_{1}}{(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}}\Big(|\hat{y}-\hat{x}|-(\hat{y}-\hat{x})_{1}\Big)\leq\frac{2\delta^{j}}{\epsilon}\frac{2K^{2}}{\epsilon}\sigma_{r}.

Combining this with (
4.13
), (
7.1
), and (
7.2
) we see that

|u^{i}-u^{i-1}|\leq(u^{i}-u^{i-1})_{1}+\left(\frac{2(d-1)\beta^{2j}}{\delta^{j}}+\frac{2c_{1}K\beta^{j}}{\epsilon}+\frac{4\delta^{j}K^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\right)\sigma_{r}\leq(u^{i}-u^{i-1})_{1}+\frac{8\delta^{j}K^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\sigma_{r}

and therefore by Theorem
1.5
, using (
4.13
) and the assumption
t\geq C_{26}K^{2}
,

\displaystyle h(|u^{i}-u^{i-1}|)

\displaystyle\leq h((u^{i}-u^{i-1})_{1})+\frac{16\mu\delta^{j}K^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\sigma_{r}\leq\mu(u^{i}-u^{i-1})_{1}+2C_{33}\sigma(2\delta^{j}r)+\frac{16\mu\delta^{j}K^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\sigma_{r}

(7.3)

\displaystyle\leq\mu(u^{i}-u^{i-1})_{1}+\frac{c_{2}\delta^{j\chi_{1}}K^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\sigma_{r}\leq\mu(u^{i}-u^{i-1})_{1}+\frac{\lambda^{j}}{2}t\sigma_{r}.

It follows using Lemma
3.2
that

\displaystyle P\Big(\hat{T}(u^{i-1},u^{i})-\mu(u^{i}-u^{i-1})_{1}\geq\lambda^{j}t\sigma_{r}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq P\left(\hat{T}(u^{i-1},u^{i})-h(|u^{i}-u^{i-1}|)\geq\frac{\lambda^{j}}{2}t\sigma_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{44}\exp\left(-C_{45}\frac{\lambda^{j}t\sigma_{r}}{\sigma(2\delta^{j}r)}\right)

(7.4)

\displaystyle\leq C_{44}\exp\left(-c_{3}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j}t\right).

The same is valid for a final link, with
j=j_{1}
. For macroscopic links it is valid with
\sigma(2\delta^{j}r)
replaced by
\sigma_{r}
, giving

(7.5)

P\Big(\hat{T}(u^{i-1},u^{i})-\mu(u^{i}-u^{i-1})_{1}\geq\lambda t\sigma_{r}\Big)\leq C_{44}e^{-c_{4}\lambda t}.

The numbers of possible links inside
G_{r}(K)
are as follows:

(i)

j
th–scale links: at most
c_{5}K^{d-1}/\delta^{2j}\beta^{2j(d-1)}
,

(ii)

final links: at most
c_{6}K^{d-1}r^{d}
,

(iii)

macroscopic links: at most
c_{7}K^{d-1}/\delta^{2}\beta^{2(d-1)}
.

Since
t\geq C_{26}K^{2}
, provided
C_{26}
is taken large enough it follows from these bounds and (
4.14
), (
7
), (
7.5
) that

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Big(\text{for some $\hat{x},\hat{y}\in G_{r}(K)$ and $j\leq j_{1}$, there is a $j$th--scale link $(v,w)$ in $\Omega_{xy}$}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{satisfying }\hat{T}(v,w)-\mu(w-v)_{1}\geq\lambda^{j}t\sigma_{r}\Big)\leq c_{8}\exp\left(-c_{10}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j}t\right),

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Big(\text{for some $\hat{x},\hat{y}\in G_{r}(K)$ there is a final link $(v,w)$ in $\Omega_{xy}$ }

\displaystyle\qquad\text{satisfying }\hat{T}(v,w)-\mu(w-v)_{1}\geq\lambda^{j_{1}}t\sigma_{r}\Big)\leq c_{8}\exp\left(-c_{10}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\delta^{\chi_{1}}}\right)^{j_{1}}t\right),

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\Big(\text{for some $\hat{x},\hat{y}\in G_{r}(K)$ the macroscopic link $(v,w)$ in $\Omega_{xy}$ }

(7.6)

\displaystyle\qquad\text{satisfies }\hat{T}(v,w)-\mu(w-v)_{1}\geq\lambda t\sigma_{r}\Big)\leq c_{8}e^{-c_{10}\lambda t}.

If
\omega
is not in any of the events in (
7
), and
\omega\notin J^{(0)}(c_{29})
, then for all
x,y
as in (
1.12
),

\displaystyle T(x,y)=T(\hat{x},\hat{y})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n+1}\hat{T}(u^{i-1},u^{i})+c_{29}n\log r

\displaystyle\leq\mu(y-x)_{1}+c_{29}j_{1}\log r+(\lambda+\lambda^{j_{1}})t\sigma_{r}+\sum_{j=1}^{j_{1}}\lambda^{j}t\sigma_{r}

\displaystyle\leq h(|y-x|)+\frac{2\lambda}{1-\lambda}t\sigma_{r}.

Taking
\lambda<1/3
it follows that

P\Big(T(x,y)-ET(x,y)\geq t\sigma_{r}\text{ for some $x,y\in G_{r}(K)$ with }|y-x|\geq\epsilon r\Big)\leq c_{10}e^{-c_{11}t},

which completes the proof of Theorem
1.3
for downward deviations.

8.
Proof of Theorem
1.6
and Lemma
1.2
.

We finish with (5) of Remark
1.7
by proving Theorem
1.6
. In the proof in section
5
for fixed
x,y
, transverse wandering cases (i)–(iii) were dealt with uniformly over
x,y
, so we need only consider the case there:

(iv)
s\leq c_{0}(\log r)^{1/2}
and there exist
x,y,u\in\mathbb{V}
with

(8.1)

x,y\in G_{r}(K),\quad u\notin G_{r,s},\quad d(u,G_{r,s})\leq 2\quad|y-x|>r_{0},\quad u\in\Gamma_{xy},\quad u_{1}\in[x_{1},y_{1}].

See the dashed line in Figure
10
.
As in section
5
, this means that, for
C_{56}
from Lemma
3.6
,

|u-x|+|y-u|-|y-x|\geq s^{2}\sigma_{r},\quad\text{so}\quad h(|u-x|)+h(|y-u|)-h(|y-x|)\geq\frac{\mu s^{2}}{2}\sigma_{r}-C_{56}

while

T(x,u)+T(u,y)-T(x,y)=0

so by Theorem
1.3
and Remark
1.4
,

\displaystyle P\Big((iv)\text{ holds}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq P\left(\text{there exist $v,w\in G_{r}(2s)$ with }\big|T(v,w)-h(|w-v|)\big|\geq\frac{\mu s^{2}}{7}\sigma_{r}\right)

(8.2)

\displaystyle\leq C_{36}e^{-c_{1}s^{2}}.

Together with cases (i)–(iii) in section
5
, this completes the proof of Theorem
1.6
.

Proof of Lemma
1.2
.

Fix
M
(large) and
\epsilon>0
, and define

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f(r)}=\log\rho(e^{r})-\chi r,\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\beta_{k}}=\sup\{f(r):2^{k-1}M<r\leq 2^{k}M\},

so
f(r)=o(r)
and hence
\beta_{k}=o(2^{k})
. Letting

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}a_{k}}=\frac{(2^{k-1}+2^{k})M}{2},

we define
\tilde{f}
as follows.
First let
\tilde{f}\equiv\beta_{1}
on
(0,a_{1}]
. Then for those
k\geq 1
with
\beta_{k}\geq\beta_{k+1}
, define
\tilde{f}
on
(a_{k},a_{k+1}]
by

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\tilde{f}(t)}=\begin{cases}\beta_{k}&\text{if }t\in(a_{k},2^{k}M]\\
\beta_{k+1}&\text{if }t=a_{k+1}\\
\text{linear on }[2^{k}M,a_{k+1}].\end{cases}

For
k\geq 1
with
\beta_{k}<\beta_{k+1}
, define
\tilde{f}
on
(a_{k},a_{k+1}]
by

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\tilde{f}(t)}=\begin{cases}\beta_{k+1}&\text{if }t\in[2^{k}M,a_{k+1}]\\
\beta_{k}&\text{if }t=a_{k}\\
\text{linear on }[a_{k},2^{k}M].\end{cases}

Since
\beta_{k}=o(2^{k})
it is easily seen that if we take
M
sufficiently large, then the slope of the piecewise–linear function
\tilde{f}
is never more than
\epsilon
, and the slope at
r
approaches 0 as
r\to\infty
. Defining
\tilde{\rho}(r)
by

\log{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\tilde{\rho}(e^{r})}=\tilde{f}(r)+\chi r

it follows that for all
s\geq r>1
,

\frac{\log\tilde{\rho}(s)-\log\tilde{\rho}(r)}{\log s-\log r}=\frac{\tilde{f}(\log s)-\tilde{f}(\log r)}{\log s-\log r}+\chi\in[\chi-\epsilon,\chi+\epsilon],

and the sublinearly powerlike property follows.
∎
